# TUI inventory: goodvibes-tui to products/tui and packages/engine

Every tracked file of goodvibes-tui (1618 files, from `git -C ~/Projects/goodvibes-tui ls-files`), read in full, with its disposition and, for a JEV or HOIST file, its decision points. Nothing is dropped unless it is specific to WRFC (the old write, review, fix, confirm chain: workstream orchestration, attempt judging, the WRFC panel format, WRFC persistence, the WRFC agent guard) or specific to QEMU sandboxing. The intent document's other DROP calls for this product are void per the owner ruling of 2026-09-26: the planning, work-plan, workstream, review and codebase command runtimes it names as DROP carry forward instead, either as JEV onto the contract runner or as plain PORT, once read and confirmed to hold no WRFC or QEMU content, matching the precedent already set for goodvibes-daemon's `legacy-daemon-migration.ts` and `legacy-daemon-reconcile.ts` in docs/inventory/daemon.md. TUI views of WRFC-era state (chain panels, review summaries, workstream views, and the workstream notification) become views or notifications of the contract tree: these are JEV, noted as such, never DROP and never a plain PORT, since the screen looks the same but the data model underneath is now the contract tree. Permission mode, the eval registry, cost tracking, the user error formatter, natural-language schedule parsing, payment card intake, the recall commands, policy and policy dispatch, and a handful of other files the intent document marks HOIST elsewhere in its platform tables turn up here too, on the product side of that same move, named against the engine subsystem they land in. The UI is not redesigned: every screen, panel, command, key binding, style and interaction matches the old product exactly. docs/inventory/daemon.md and docs/inventory/agent.md are the matching inventories for the sibling daemon and agent products, docs/inventory/engine.md is the matching inventory for packages/engine (from goodvibes-sdk), and docs/inventory/wrfc-to-jev.md maps every WRFC function to its Jev form.

Read, understand, choose (owner ruling 2026-09-27): each row names every check its file makes (each list, set, regex, pattern table, cutoff or threshold, lookup, comparison or classification whose result decides what the code does next), with its line, what it decides and how it decides it today, and the choice made on that reading. A check that answers a question of meaning with a guess becomes a Jev reading, named by pattern and battery; a check stays code only with the concrete reason it is not a guess (id equality, arithmetic, a size or limit the program sets, a grammar a machine produced, a closed enum this program writes, a key event compared to a configured binding, a value the owner configured, exactly what the user typed). A category label (deterministic, fixed format, security, display only, renders already-judged state) is never the reason to keep a check as code, and a blanket conversion is never the reason to make one a Jev reading: security checks, boundary lists, taint, trust tiers, sanitizers, redaction and format detection get the same reading as everything else. Where a check's engine counterpart is already decided in docs/inventory/engine.md, the row cites that engine row. No heuristic is kept behind a Jev reading, not even as a backup, and what a site does when Jev cannot answer is left to the owner.

Correction (coordinator, 2026-09-27): WRFC code is JEV in the contract runner, as in the engine inventory, so the WRFC guard, panel format, persistence, reviewer rubric and their tests are JEV below; DROP is kept only for the three QEMU files and the literal WRFC run artifact with its test.

Files: 1618. PORT 1419, JEV 110, HOIST 84, DROP 5. Decision points: 62.

L248-265 `isReadOnlyTask` decides whether a task changes nothing; now do-not-write phrase regexes and a leading-analysis-verb regex; choice: Jev `contract.unit-shape` `role` (research or design, changing nothing). L156-160 `shouldRouteSpawnToWrfc` and L162-166 `shouldCollapseBatchToAuthoritativeWrfc` decide chain routing and batch collapse by composing the guesses above; choice: code composition over those readings (the agent tool starts one contract for a batch and its tasks become proposed units, per wrfc-to-jev.md). ### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/export/gist-uploader.ts:58-59` (`key.toLowerCase().includes('token')`) | Which of a configured service's auth headers carries the GitHub token, by whether the header name contains the substring `token`; misses credential headers named otherwise and takes any header whose name contains the word. | Jev `engine.tools.credential-header` (yes/no per header name: does this header, by its name, carry a credential); the first header with an acting yes supplies the token. |
| `src/permissions/sandbox-exec-gate.ts:129` (`decideSandboxedExec`) | Whether a sandboxed exec command needs network access or host privilege escalation, read inside the old SDK policy from the normalization classifier's fixed network and privilege command-name sets | `engine.gate.sandbox-needs` yes/no pair (needsNetwork, needsPrivilege): does this command need network access; does it need host privilege escalation; read per command by `readCommandNeeds` and passed as `needs` to `decideSandboxedExec` (already implemented in the engine) |
| `src/tools/wrfc-agent-guard.ts:219` (`containsWrfcSignal`) | Whether an agent task asks for reviewed chain work, by the regex `\bwrfc\b\|work-review-fix` over the task text | `contract.request-route` dispatch: is the request conversation, a question answered by reading, or work that runs as a contract (already implemented in the engine, read once per turn by intake-route.ts) |
| `src/tools/wrfc-agent-guard.ts:224` (`isRootReviewRoleTask`) | Whether a proposed task is a reviewer, tester or verifier role, by a template-name regex and role and verb regexes over the task text | `contract.unit-shape` `role` choice: implement, research, design, review, test or verify; review, test and verify units are refused (already implemented in the engine) |
| `src/tools/wrfc-agent-guard.ts:239` (`isImplementationLikeTask`) | Whether a spawned task is implementation work, by a list of about 40 action verbs | `contract.unit-shape` `role` choice: does the unit change files or produce the deliverable (implement) (already implemented in the engine) |
| `src/tools/wrfc-agent-guard.ts:248` (`isReadOnlyTask`) | Whether a task changes nothing, by do-not-write phrase regexes and a leading analysis-verb regex | `contract.unit-shape` `role` choice: research or design, changing nothing (already implemented in the engine) |
| `src/tools/wrfc-agent-guard.ts:104` (`selectAuthoritativeTask`) | Whether the model's proposed task narrowed the user's ask, answered by always replacing it with the user's last message when one exists | `contract.unit-shape` `narrows` yes/no: does the proposed unit drop or narrow anything the goal requires (already implemented in the engine) |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/panels/agent-inspector-shared.ts:33` (`countStalledAgents`) | Whether a non-terminal agent is stalled, by 5 minutes elapsed since it started | Jev yes/no, new products/tui battery: has this agent stopped making progress, read from its latest activity record |
| `src/panels/modals/memory-modal.ts:93` (`rankForReview`) | Review-queue order by hand weights: fresh +40, stale +20, contradicted +10, 100 minus confidence, flagged states -20, then recency (a client copy of the SDK `reviewQueueScore`) | `engine.state.memory-review-priority` item `needs_review` (low band): does this record need review, ordered by probability; already implemented in the engine as rankReviewQueue (per engine.md `sdk/src/platform/state/memory-store.ts` row); the modal shows the engine's ranked queue |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/runtime/bootstrap.ts:223` (`getSystemPrompt`, `getTierForContextWindow`) | Which tier guidance to append to the system prompt, guessed from the current model's context window through two thresholds | Implemented in the engine: `routing.model-tier` through `readTierPromptSupplement` (engine.md `sdk/src/platform/providers/tier-prompts.ts:22` decision row), remembered per model; the port reads it when the model is set and `getSystemPrompt` appends the stored supplement |
| `src/runtime/process-lifecycle.ts:167` (`unhandledRejectionHandler`, `formatUserFacingError`) | Whether an unhandled rejection is an authentication, rate-limit, context-window or network failure (its own `[Error]` line) or generic, guessed by `classifyError`'s regex ladder over the error's status, name, message and cause | Done in E.4: `readUserFacingError` (packages/engine/sdk/src/platform/routing/user-error.ts L156): 401, 429 and the errno table stay code, the wording is read by `engine.failure-reading` fanned out with `routing.user-error`; the port awaits it in the handler |
| `src/test/runtime/compaction/quality-score.test.ts:14` (`computeQualityScore`, engine `sdk/src/platform/runtime/compaction/quality-score.ts:142`) | How much of the conversation a compaction kept and whether it is low quality, guessed by marker words ('[Session', 'compaction', 'collapsed' and others) matched by substring, a 20-character non-trivial cutoff and an equal-weight checklist blended at a fixed weight; the test pins those signals and grades | Per engine.md `quality-score.ts:27`, `:123` and `:149` decision rows, done: `engine.compaction.retention` (rated rubric) and `engine.compaction.fidelity` (fidelity pattern), composed with the compression score by geometric mean against the pinned 0.4 floor; the test stubs or records both readings |
| `src/test/runtime/ecosystem/catalog.test.ts:84` (`searchEcosystemCatalog`, engine `sdk/src/platform/runtime/ecosystem/catalog.ts:374`) | Whether a curated catalog entry is what the searcher is after, guessed by containment of the lowercased query in the entry's joined id, name, summary, source, trust notes, install hint and tags | Per engine.md `catalog.ts:374` decision row, done: yes/no `engine.ecosystem.catalog-search` per (query, entry), an entry hidden only on an acting no; the test awaits the search and stubs or records the reading |
| `src/test/runtime/forensics/forensics.test.ts:130` (`classifyFailure`, engine `sdk/src/platform/runtime/forensics/classifier.ts:103`) | Whether a failed turn's or task's free-text error message means a timeout or a failed LLM call, guessed by keyword lists ('timeout', 'timed out', 'rate limit', 'overloaded', '503' and others); the test pins those words | Per engine.md `classifier.ts:103` decision row, done: `engine.failure-reading` (one reading per wording, shared with the retry and display paths) mapped by the fixed `CLASS_FOR_CATEGORY` table; the test stubs or records the reading |
| `src/test/runtime/forensics/forensics.test.ts:451` (`ForensicsCollector`, engine `sdk/src/platform/runtime/forensics/collector.ts:600`) | Which timed phases of a failed turn or task were slow, guessed by one fixed 1000 ms line for every phase kind | Per engine.md `collector.ts:600` decision row, done: yes/no `engine.runtime.forensics-slow-phase` per timed phase when the report is built; the collector cases supply its answers |
| `src/test/runtime/mcp/permissions-coherence.test.ts:8` (`McpPermissionManager.evaluateToolCall`, engine `sdk/src/platform/runtime/mcp/permissions.ts:66`) | An MCP call's capability class and risk, guessed by keyword matching over the tool name and a fixed capability-to-risk table; the test pins `write_fs`, `exec`, `medium` and the resulting verdicts | Per engine.md `permissions.ts` row, done: `engine.gate.side-effect` item `capability` at site `engine.mcp.capability` for the class and the gate's `readToolCall` (`engine.gate.side-effect`, `engine.gate.risk-family`, `engine.gate.boundary`) for the stakes; the role table and scopes stay code; the test awaits the call and stubs or records the readings |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/input/commands/schedule-nl.ts:67` | `parseNaturalLanguageSchedule` turns a free-text scheduling phrase ("every weekday at 9am", "in 2 hours", "daily", "every monday at 08:00") into a cron/every/at schedule via a hand-written ladder of regexes and keyword/weekday-name lookup tables (`DOW`, `UNIT_MS`, the `every`/`in`/`at` phrase matchers). | JEV date-parts reading: extract the schedule's structured parts (kind: cron/every/at, plus the numeric interval/time-of-day/weekday components) from the natural-language phrase, per intent's "To the engine scheduler, as a JEV date-parts reading." |
| `src/input/commands/teamwork-runtime.ts:101` | `classifyArchetype` lowercases an agent archetype's name+description into a haystack string and matches keyword substrings ("review"/"audit" → review; "test" or has exec+write-without-edit → test; "research"/"analysis" or has find+analyze-without-write/edit → research; has write or edit → implement; else general) to decide the archetype's family, which in turn sets its `reviewMode` (`'wrfc'` only for the review family). | Choice-over-options battery: which family (review, test, research, implement, or general) best describes this agent archetype, given its name, description, and declared tools. |
| `src/input/commands/planning-runtime.ts:228` | The `/project-plan answer <question> <answer>` subcommand submits a free-text answer to an open planning question via `projectPlanningService.answerQuestion(...)`, whose evaluation of whether the answer resolves the open question is exactly the site intent names. | JEV yes/no reading, per intent's own naming: "To the contract runner, as a JEV yes/no reading: project planning answer actions": does this answer resolve the open planning question, yes/no. |
| `src/test/input/planning-runtime-command.test.ts` (exercising `src/input/commands/planning-runtime.ts:228`) | The `/project-plan answer <question> <answer>` subcommand submits a free-text answer to an open planning question via `projectPlanningService.answerQuestion(...)`, whose evaluation of whether the answer resolves the open question is exactly the site intent names. | JEV yes/no reading, per intent's own naming: "To the contract runner, as a JEV yes/no reading: project planning answer actions": does this answer resolve the open planning question, yes/no. |
| `src/test/input/schedule-nl.test.ts` (exercising `src/input/commands/schedule-nl.ts:67`) | `parseNaturalLanguageSchedule` turns a free-text scheduling phrase ("every weekday at 9am", "in 2 hours", "daily", "every monday at 08:00") into a cron/every/at schedule via a hand-written ladder of regexes and keyword/weekday-name lookup tables. | JEV date-parts reading: extract the schedule's structured parts (kind: cron/every/at, plus the numeric interval/time-of-day/weekday components) from the natural-language phrase, per intent's "To the engine scheduler, as a JEV date-parts reading." |
| `src/test/input/product-breadth-commands.test.ts` (exercising `src/input/commands/teamwork-runtime.ts:101`, same site as `teamwork-runtime.test.ts`) | The test's `/teamwork archetypes` assertion drives a real custom agent-definition file (description "Documentation specialist for operator handoff notes") through `classifyArchetype`, which lowercases the archetype's name+description into a haystack and matches keyword substrings ("review"/"audit" → review; "test" → test; "research"/"analysis" → research; has write or edit → implement; else general), and pins the literal expected output `implement`. | Choice-over-options battery: which family (review, test, research, implement, or general) best describes this agent archetype, given its name, description, and declared tools. |

Does not exercise `classifyArchetype` (that decision point, found by a sibling slice, lives in `teamwork-runtime.ts` and is not touched here). |

### Decision points

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `src/test/agents/context-window.test.ts:145-183` (`isContextSizeExceededError`, `platform/types`) | Matches an arbitrary provider error's message text against a list of phrases ("context_length_exceeded", "context size exceeded", "context window exceeded", "prompt is too long", "input too long", "tokens exceed") to decide whether the failure means the model's context window was exceeded. | Yes/no battery: "does this provider error mean the model's context window was exceeded". |
| `src/test/agents/score-extraction.test.ts:13-88` (`extractScoreFromText`, `platform/agents`) | Regex extraction of a numeric score (e.g. "Score: 8/10", "scored 8.5", "rated 7") from a reviewer's freeform prose. | Score-on-a-rubric battery: score the reviewed work directly, no text to parse. |
| `src/test/agents/score-extraction.test.ts:94-133` (`extractPassedFromText`, `platform/agents`) | Keyword match ("passed"/"approved"/"failed"/"passes"/"passing") over freeform review prose, combined with a score threshold, to decide pass/fail. | Judge-output-against-goal battery: does this attempt satisfy the goal, yes/no. |
| `src/test/agents/score-extraction.test.ts:139-190` (`extractIssuesFromText`, `platform/agents`) | Regex/bracket parsing of a freeform numbered or bulleted list ("1. [critical] ...", "- (major) ...") to extract severity-tagged issues from review prose. | Policy-checklist / score-on-a-rubric battery: return structured findings per dimension directly, no text to parse. |
| `src/test/integrations/github.test.ts:262-291` (`GitHubIntegration.eventToPrompt`, `platform/integrations`, issue_comment branch) | Checks whether an issue comment's body contains the literal substring "@goodvibes" to decide whether the comment addresses the assistant and should become an agent prompt. | Existence-check / yes-no battery: "does this comment address or summon the assistant". |
| `src/test/release-gates/operability-gate.test.ts:76-96,139-140` | `classifyFailure` maps a free-text `errorMessage` string ("request timed out after 30s", "API error 503 service unavailable", "network failure: ECONNRESET") to a `FailureClass` category by substring matching, alongside its structural boolean/enum cases. | Choice-over-fixed-options battery / routing pattern: which failure class (timeout, LLM/provider error, tool failure, etc.) does this error message describe, over the closed `FailureClass` set. |
| `src/test/release-gates/performance-gate.test.ts:177-186` | `computeQualityScore` computes a hand-tuned numeric score and applies `LOW_QUALITY_THRESHOLD` (0.4) over compaction input/output signals (token counts, handoff presence, message-count sanity) to decide `isLowQuality` and a letter grade. | Fidelity check pattern (named directly by the intent document: "compaction quality uses the fidelity pattern"). |
| `src/test/tools/registry-tool.test.ts:207-219` | The `recommend` mode's ranking sorts skills/tools by keyword overlap between a free-text `task` description (e.g. "review code quality") and each candidate's name/description, to decide display order. | Rerank pattern (named directly by the intent document for the skills subsystem: "relevance by rerank"). |

| `docs/wrfc/wrfc-a7c9e318-engineer-report.json` | DROP | A literal WRFC-era generated artifact: an actual persisted engineer-report run output (summary, gatheredContext, plannedActions, appliedChanges, filesCreated/Modified, decisions, issues) for chain `wrfc-a7c9e318`, checked in as WRFC chain-run evidence. This is WRFC run-output data itself, not prose explaining WRFC's history, so it falls squarely in the WRFC-specific generated-artifact DROP carve-out. |
| `examples/reference-http-client/index.ts` | PORT | Minimal reference client demonstrating the HTTP transport against the operator/peer surfaces (provider snapshot, control-plane snapshot, node-host contract). Plain async calls and env-var reads, no natural-language guesswork. |

| `examples/reference-node-host/README.md` | PORT | Usage doc for the reference node-host client: pairing/heartbeat/work-pull lifecycle, built-in work-type handlers, command allowlisting. Pure prose. |
| `examples/reference-node-host/src/cli.ts` | PORT | CLI entry point for the reference node-host example: argv parsing by exact flag/subcommand matching (`--config`, `contract`/`pair`/`verify`/`once`/`start`) and JSON config loading. Structural command dispatch over a fixed, closed set of subcommands, not natural-language guesswork. |
| `examples/reference-node-host/src/index.ts` | PORT | Barrel export for the reference node-host client. Matches the daemon precedent: published entry points and reference material are PORT. |
| `examples/reference-node-host/src/reference-node-host.ts` | PORT | Reference implementation of a distributed node-host client (pairing, heartbeat, work pull/complete) demonstrating the SDK's remote-peer surface. Every branch is a closed-set `work.type` dispatch (`status.request`/`location.request`/`session.message`/`automation.run`/`invoke`) and an exact command-allowlist membership check, no free-text classification. |
| `examples/reference-node-host/test/reference-node-host.test.ts` | PORT | Test for the reference client against a stub HTTP server; exact request/response fixtures. |
| `examples/reference-operator-client/index.ts` | PORT | Smallest in-process reference consumer of the runtime services / direct transport / operator surface. Reference material, no decision points. |
| `examples/reference-operator-client/README.md` | PORT | Documents the reference operator client example. |
| `.github/actions/setup/action.yml` | PORT | Composite CI action: pins Bun, restores cache, installs deps. Fixed YAML/shell mechanics. |
| `.github/workflows/action-self-test.yml` | PORT | Non-gating workflow that installs the published GitHub Action wrapper and exercises `doctor --json`; fixed job graph, exit-code contract explicitly not masked. |
| `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | PORT | Main CI pipeline: typecheck, test, coverage gate, architecture check, perf check, eval gate, build+smoke, package gate, and an auto-release job that tags and dispatches release.yml on green main. All fixed job graph and shell conditionals. |
| `.github/workflows/release.yml` | PORT | Release pipeline: tag-version verification, by-reference per-job-green re-verification, binary matrix, GH release, staged npm/GitHub Packages publish with kill switches. Fixed job graph. |
| `.github/workflows/windows-beta.yml` | PORT | Non-gating beta Windows build+smoke workflow, weekly and on-demand. Fixed job graph. |
| `.gitignore` | PORT | Standard ignore list plus this project's own test-scratch/tsbuildinfo/probe-file entries. |
| `.goodvibes/agents/reviewer.md` | JEV | JEV in the contract runner (docs/inventory/wrfc-to-jev.md, docs/design/contract-runner.md): the reviewer rubric's evidence rules become the evidence the runner collects for each unit and the judge's criteria; the file is rewritten as the runner's evidence guidance. |
| `.goodvibes/eval/baseline.json` | PORT | Eval-suite score baseline (`gate:*` scenario scores) consumed by `scripts/eval-gate.ts`'s regression gate. Not WRFC-related; plain scenario-scoring data, not a coverage-percentage gate (not Supercov). |
| `.goodvibes/GOODVIBES.md` | PORT | This repo's own AI-assistant working-style directives (token-usage minimization, batching, multi-agent execution). One bullet ("NEVER skip WRFC without explicit user confirmation") mentions WRFC in passing among many unrelated directives; the file's content and purpose are not specifically about WRFC as its operating procedure, so the WRFC carve-out does not apply. Carries over as this project's own tooling configuration. |
| `.goodvibes/.npmignore` | PORT | Deliberately-empty file that pins `.goodvibes/`'s npm packing to package.json's `files` allowlist regardless of a local `.goodvibes/.gitignore`. Packaging mechanics only. |
| `.goodvibes/skills/add-provider/SKILL.md` | PORT | A shipped agent skill document walking a user through adding a custom LLM provider (conversational field collection, validation rules, JSON schema, smart defaults for a fixed list of known provider names). The "smart defaults" step matches a user-typed name against a closed, enumerated set of known providers (ollama/together/openrouter/groq/lm-studio/fireworks/vllm), an exact/enum match, not a free-text classification heuristic; the surrounding conversational collection is delegated to the agent's own judgment already, not a hand-tuned heuristic in code. No WRFC/QEMU content. |

| `README.md` | PORT | Product overview, install instructions, feature tour, configuration reference, development notes. |
| `scripts/assemble-platform-packages.ts` | PORT | Release-time step copying built binaries + sqlite-vec addon into each platform package's `bin/` and stamping its version. Deterministic file/version mechanics. |
| `scripts/audit-goodvibes-home.ts` | PORT | CLI wrapper around the SDK's `.goodvibes` home audit, rendering Markdown/JSON. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/bun-compile-compat.ts` | PORT | Prebuild step patching known Bun-compile incompatibilities in vendored deps via exact source-string replacement. |
| `scripts/check-architecture.ts` | PORT | CI static-analysis gate: line-count cap, pattern-based rules over code syntax, explicit-`any` detection via the TS compiler API, import-cycle detection (Tarjan SCC), layer-boundary rules, hex-literal/selected-index/unused-export/internal-identifier ratchets, and SDK contract-catalog invariants. Every rule matches source-code shape or a fixed machine grammar, never free-text meaning; matches the daemon's `check-architecture.ts` precedent exactly. |
| `scripts/check-bun.sh` | PORT | `preinstall` guard: refuses install when `bun` is not on PATH. |
| `scripts/check-workflows.ts` | PORT | Structural validation of GitHub Actions workflow YAML (parses, checks required fields, bans job-level `continue-on-error`, checks release-workflow job requirements). Structural/schema checks over YAML keys, not free text. |
| `scripts/coverage-gate.ts` | PORT | Aggregate whole-suite coverage gate, a thin adapter over the shared toolchain's coverage-summary parser and floor comparison, sourced from `toolchain.config.json`. A plain bun-test coverage-percentage gate ported from the daemon; not the named external Supercov tool, so the "Supercov is not part of this project" rule does not apply. |
| `scripts/eval-gate.ts` | PORT | CI eval gate: runs the standing-gate scenario suites, compares against a stored baseline, fails on regression beyond threshold or an absolute floor. Scenario-scoring regression gate, not a coverage-percentage gate; unrelated to Supercov. |
| `scripts/export-foundation-artifacts.ts` | PORT | Thin entry point invoking `syncFoundationArtifacts` under the workspace lock. |
| `scripts/generate-command-reference.ts` | PORT | Regenerates `docs/commands-reference.md` from the live slash-command registry. |
| `scripts/hex-literal-baseline.json` | PORT | Seeded per-file ratchet baseline (allowed raw hex-color-literal counts) consumed by `hex-literal-rule.ts`. Pure data table. (One entry names `src/panels/wrfc-panel-format.ts`, a file outside this slice whose own disposition is handled elsewhere; this baseline file itself is generic ratchet data.) |
| `scripts/hex-literal-rule.ts` | PORT | Architecture-gate rule banning raw hex color literals outside the theme-token source files, enforced as a ratchet against the seeded baseline. Exact regex over a fixed machine syntax (hex color codes), not free-text classification. |
| `scripts/install.sh` | PORT | The public one-line suite installer (`curl \| sh`): resolves per-repository release tags, downloads and checksum-verifies binaries for the daemon/TUI/agent/webui, manages systemd/launchd service units by exact unit-provenance markers, and detects/removes PATH-shadowing copies by an exact `"<command> <dotted-version>"` output-shape match. Every judgment call in it is explicitly built to refuse rather than guess (the file's own comments repeatedly say "no guess," "recognizably platform-managed," "exact shape"); all checks are structural/exact-match over machine state, not natural-language meaning. |
| `scripts/internal-identifier-rule.ts` | PORT | Architecture-gate rule banning internal planning-identifier shorthand (wave/work-order/debt/UX ids, lettered finding ids) from tracked text via a fixed set of regexes over exact machine-defined token shapes, not prose meaning. |
| `scripts/leak-scan.ts` | PORT | Runs the whole suite in one process with a timer-leak detector preloaded, reporting still-live handles. Deterministic harness. |
| `scripts/no-unused-exports-rule.ts` | PORT | Architecture-gate rule banning renderer exports with no non-test import site, via TypeScript AST parsing and cross-file import-binding resolution. Structural analysis, not free-text classification. |
| `scripts/package-install-check.ts` | PORT | Static "as it would install" verification: shared tarball/bin-shim policy (toolchain) plus TUI-specific bin-fallback verification. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/perf-baseline.json` | PORT | Ratchet perf budgets (startup/frame/line-production timings) consumed by `perf-check.ts`. Pure data. |
| `scripts/perf-check.ts` | PORT | CI performance-budget gate: headless startup/frame/line-production measurements compared against committed ratchet budgets. Numeric threshold comparisons over measured timings, not text classification. |
| `scripts/perf-coalesce-bench.ts` | PORT | Headless before/after measurement harness for render-call coalescing. Not a gate. |
| `scripts/perf-frame-bench.ts` | PORT | Shared headless Compositor.composite() throughput benchmark, consumed by both the gate and a release-gate test. |
| `scripts/perf-line-bench.ts` | PORT | Headless line-production micro-benchmarks (transcript build/append/resize, panel build, markdown/code-block/overlay render) with GC-bracketed heap accounting. Pure timing/allocation measurement. |
| `scripts/platform-packages.ts` | PORT | Shared descriptor table for the four `@pellux/goodvibes-tui-<os>-<arch>` platform packages, consumed by assemble/publish/sync scripts. Pure data. |
| `scripts/postinstall.d.ts` | PORT | Type declarations for the plain-JS `postinstall.js`, re-exported from the runtime module it wraps so the two can never disagree. |
| `scripts/postinstall.js` | PORT | npm postinstall: installs the platform binary (from the matching optional platform package, or a checksum-verified download), deploys bundled `.goodvibes` skills/agents/GOODVIBES.md to the user's home on first install (skip-if-exists), and provisions the wake-word model (never fails the install). Deterministic file/network mechanics; the "deploy bundled files" step would stop copying `.goodvibes/agents/reviewer.md` once that file is dropped from the source tree, but this script's own logic needs no change. |
| `scripts/prebuild.ts` | PORT | Prebuild entry point: patches Bun-compile compatibility and syncs project surfaces under the workspace lock. |
| `scripts/project-surfaces.ts` | PORT | Syncs version strings into `src/version.ts`/README, regenerates foundation artifacts (operator/peer contract JSON, knowledge GraphQL/SQL schema) and the command-reference doc from the live SDK/command registry. Deterministic generation and exact regex substitution on fixed string shapes (a version number, a badge URL), not natural-language classification. |
| `scripts/publish-check.ts` | PORT | Pre-publish gate: shared SDK-pin tri-agreement (toolchain), TUI-specific required package.json fields, shared tarball/bin-shim policy, and a registry-auth probe. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/publish-package.ts` | PORT | Stages and publishes the main npm package (with idempotent re-publish handling and optional registry-scope rewriting for the GitHub Packages mirror). Deterministic. |
| `scripts/publish-platform-packages.ts` | PORT | Publishes the assembled platform binary packages, idempotently, with optional scope rescoping for a mirror registry. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/release-gate.ts` | PORT | Runs the five release-gate test suites (safety/determinism/performance/operability/product) and reports pass/fail per gate. Thin test-suite orchestrator, no decision points. |
| `scripts/release.ts` | PORT | Local release-cut wrapper: assembles the CHANGELOG body (from an explicit release-notes env var, refusing raw commit-hash lines via a fixed-shape guard, or falling back to the git log for dry runs) and invokes the shared release-cut tool. The commit-hash-line check is a fixed-shape guard against a known failure mode, not a natural-language judgment about note quality (same reasoning as the daemon's `release.ts`). |
| `scripts/run-tests.ts` | PORT | Parallel per-file test runner: per-file TMPDIR isolation, stale-tmp sweeps, optional substring pattern filter, per-file timeout ceiling. Deterministic orchestration. |
| `scripts/sdk-dev.ts` | PORT | Thin forwarder to the canonical SDK-overlay dev tool in the sibling SDK checkout. |
| `scripts/selected-index-rule.ts` | PORT | Architecture-gate rule banning the raw `[this.selectedIndex]` token in panel source (a real bug class: indexing the raw item array instead of the filtered visible list) via exact-string search over source syntax. Not free-text classification. |
| `scripts/stale-tmp-sweep.ts` | PORT | Age-gated cleanup of stale test-scratch directories under `.test-tmp` and, by an exact enumerated prefix list, the real OS temp directory. Exact-prefix matching, not a heuristic guess. |
| `scripts/sync-platform-optional-deps.ts` | PORT | Release-time step re-stamping the root package's platform `optionalDependencies` versions to match the bumped root version. Deterministic. |
| `scripts/test-pattern-rule.ts` | PORT | Pure argv-parsing helper extracting the optional test-file substring filter, skipping the runner's own known flags. |
| `scripts/test-temp-manifest.ts` | PORT | Handover mechanism between a finished test process and the runner: reads/writes a JSON manifest of temp directories a child owned so the parent can remove them after exit. |
| `scripts/typecheck-coverage-rule.ts` | PORT | Gate rule asserting every tracked TypeScript file is resolved by some checked tsconfig project, via `tsc --listFilesOnly`. Structural, not free-text. |
| `scripts/verification-ledger.ts` | PORT | CLI wrapper rendering the verification ledger as Markdown/JSON. |
| `scripts/verify-live.ts` | PORT | CLI wrapper building and rendering the live-verification report against a running daemon/binary. |
| `scripts/verify-release-tag-version.ts` | PORT | Verifies the pushed/dispatched git tag equals `v${package.json version}`; exact string comparison, ported from the daemon's script of the same name. |
| `scripts/workspace-lock.ts` | PORT | File-based mutual-exclusion lock with staleness detection by age and by checking whether the owning pid is still alive. Deterministic. |
| `toolchain.config.json` | PORT | Shared-toolchain configuration: build targets, coverage floors, publish policy, release-cut settings, smoke-check strings. Pure data. |
| `tsconfig.json` | PORT | TypeScript compiler configuration for the main source tree. |
| `tsconfig.test.json` | PORT | TypeScript compiler configuration for the test tree. |
| `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` | PORT | Committed TypeScript incremental-build cache artifact; no behavior of its own. |
